## 2026-06-28T22:20:00-04:00 — Codex Full PRD completion gate closure

  completion claim for repository-owned implementation, while keeping external
  customer credentials, deployment, and legal/safety approvals listed as
  non-repo prerequisites.

  2026-06-28T22:33-04:00 after the final completion-gate edits.

## 2026-06-28T21:35:00-04:00 — Codex Runner mTLS certificate alignment

- Branch: `codex/runner-mtls-certificate-alignment`.
- Reconciled `PRD-RUNNER-003`: runner registration/rotation now issue
  tenant-scoped mTLS client certificates from runner CSRs, store certificate
  SHA-256 fingerprints, and retain outbound HTTPS signed-task polling with
  bearer-token defense in depth.
- Added API support for TLS-terminator forwarded runner certificate fingerprint
  enforcement via `PERISCAN_RUNNER_REQUIRE_MTLS=true`; runner-authenticated
  calls compare the presented fingerprint to the stored runner certificate hash.
- Updated Go runner config to generate the client private key locally and load
  mTLS CA/client certificate/client key files without introducing reverse SSH,
  inbound listeners, arbitrary tunnels, or shell execution.
- Validation: `pnpm verify` PASS. Runner Go tests passed through
  `scripts/test-runner.sh` and `scripts/test-runner-lab.sh` using the Docker Go
  fallback because the local host still reports `go: command not found`.
- Follow-up documentation: `docs/COMPLETION_REPORT.md` and
  `docs/PRODUCT_COMPLETION_PLAN.md` now cite the current runner mTLS validation
  run and keep the report scoped to first-customer readiness, not a full-PRD
  completion claim.

- v0.1.191 (Onboarding/recovery increment 5: ACCOUNT LOCKOUT — TRACK COMPLETE): per-user login lockout to slow online password guessing (complements the IP/tenant rate limiter). prisma User += failedLoginAttempts Int @default(0) + lockedUntil DateTime? + AuditEventAction += login_failed (migration 20260614040000_add_login_lockout; applied 5434+generate). shared AuditEventActionSchema += login.failed; AUDIT_ACTION_TO_DB += it. auth.ts login: config LOGIN_MAX_ATTEMPTS (env PERISCAN_LOGIN_MAX_ATTEMPTS default 5) + LOGIN_LOCKOUT_MS (PERISCAN_LOGIN_LOCKOUT_MINUTES default 15); unknown/null-pw user → generic null (no lock state, no enumeration); locked (lockedUntil>now) → 429 account_locked (skips password check); wrong password → increment attempts, audit login.failed{attempts,locked}, at threshold set lockedUntil + 429; success → clear failedLoginAttempts/lockedUntil. No UserSchema/DTO change (security fields internal). .env.example += the two vars. FIX: db/src/repositories.test.ts prisma User mock += failedLoginAttempts:0, lockedUntil:null. acceptance login-lockout-flow.test.ts (max=3 via env: 2 wrong→401, 3rd→429 account_locked, correct-while-locked→429, clear lockedUntil→correct→200 + counter reset). - v0.1.192 (Operational Hardening increment A: API GRACEFUL SHUTDOWN): apps/api/src/graceful-shutdown.ts registerGracefulShutdown(server, {signals?,process?}) — on SIGTERM/SIGINT calls server.close() (Fastify drains in-flight requests + runs onClose hooks incl. the continuous-validation scheduler timer) then process.exit(0); exit(1) on close error; double-signal guard. Wired in apps/api/src/server.ts (was missing — worker + runner-agent already had shutdown). Testable helper (ClosableServer/ShutdownProcess interfaces injectable). graceful-shutdown.test.ts (4: SIGTERM→close+exit0, close-fails→exit1, second signal ignored while draining, registers default SIGTERM/SIGINT). NEXT op-hardening increments: B structured logging+correlation IDs, C security headers, D queue tenant fairness, E webhook idempotency/DLQ.
- v0.1.193 (Operational Hardening increment C: SECURITY HEADERS): zero-dependency global onSend hook in apps/api/src/app.ts (chose a hook over @fastify/helmet to avoid default-CSP breaking embedded UI + license churn; full control + exact-header tests). Every response gets X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff, X-Frame-Options: DENY, Referrer-Policy: no-referrer, Cross-Origin-Opener-Policy: same-origin, X-DNS-Prefetch-Control: off, and Content-Security-Policy "default-src 'none'; frame-ancestors 'none'; base-uri 'none'". HSTS (max-age=63072000; includeSubDomains) emitted in production only (PERISCAN_DEPLOYMENT_ENVIRONMENT=production — browsers ignore over http + avoids pinning localhost). CSP skipped only for /api/v1/api-reference (forward-compat; it's currently JSON so harmless). Test: extended app.test health test asserting the headers present + api-reference CSP-exempt + HSTS absent in test env. Gates GREEN: -r typecheck+lint, app.test 128, test:security 22, test:acceptance 54, licenses 43/27/31. (global hook → ran security+acceptance to confirm no downstream breakage). Cross-process api→worker correlation already available via runId/missionId/jobId carried in job payloads (logged per job event), so requestId-in-job threading DEFERRED as a noted follow-up (not a real gap). - v0.1.195 (Operational Hardening increment D: PER-TENANT QUEUE BACKPRESSURE): one tenant can no longer starve the shared BullMQ validation queue. mission-queue.ts: resolveQueueMaxPerTenant(env) (PERISCAN_QUEUE_MAX_PER_TENANT, null=disabled) + MissionQueue.countTenantPending?(tenantId) + BullMQ impl (bounded scan of waiting/active/delayed/prioritized jobs, cap 5000, filter data.tenantId). runtime-services.ts: assertTenantQueueCapacity({tenantId,limit,countPending,onDenied}) — injectable+unit-testable; if pending>=limit runs onDenied (audit) then throws AppServiceError(429,"queue_tenant_limit"). Wired as a PRE-CREATION guard at the start of validation.startMission + remediation.verifyRemediation (before runs/jobs created → clean 429, no garbage rows; the validation enqueue catch would otherwise mask errors as 500). onDenied writes audit queue.tenant_limited (shared AuditEventActionSchema + AUDIT_ACTION_TO_DB + prisma enum + migration 20260614050000; applied 5434+generate). Guard is a no-op unless the env cap is set. queue-capacity.test.ts (4: resolveQueueMaxPerTenant parsing, null-limit no-op never-counts, below-cap allows, at/over-cap→429+onDenied). .env.example documents PERISCAN_QUEUE_MAX_PER_TENANT. Redis-backed countTenantPending exercised in integration only (unit tests inject the count). Operator visibility: webhooks service listDeadLetteredWebhookDeliveries(context) (tenant-scoped, deadLetteredAt not null, take 100) + AppServices + GET /api/v1/tenants/current/webhook-deliveries/dead-letter + app.test stub. shared WebhookDeliverySchema += deadLetteredAt nullish; serializeWebhookDelivery emits it. delivery.test.ts (4: idempotency header=deliveryId, non-final fail no dead-letter, final fail dead-letters, final network error dead-letters); webhooks 6 tests. KEY a11y + test design: every chart ALWAYS renders a real <table> data fallback (<caption>+col/row headers) driven by the SAME data; a useChartReady() gate renders the nivo SVG only when mounted AND ResizeObserver exists — so under vitest/jsdom (no ResizeObserver, nivo can't measure) ONLY the visible table renders (testable + screen-reader accessible), while in the browser the chart shows + the table goes sr-only. - v0.1.228 (ENTERPRISE ACCESS / MFA increment 2: VERIFY+ACTIVATE + ENFORCE AT LOGIN): MFA is now real end-to-end. apps/api/src/services/auth.ts verifyMfa(context,{code}): loads user, 400 mfa_not_enrolled if no mfaSecret, decryptSecret(user.mfaSecret) + verifyTotp(secret, code, Date.now(), {window:1}); invalid→400 mfa_invalid; on first success set User.mfaEnabledAt=now + writeAuditEvent mfa.activated (idempotent — re-verify keeps original timestamp, no duplicate audit). DESIGN: an MFA failure on a CORRECT password does NOT touch the lockout counter (lockout exists to slow PASSWORD guessing; password was already valid) — documented inline. unknown-user/null-password path UNCHANGED (generic null, no enumeration). shared AuditEventActionSchema += mfa.activated; runtime-services AUDIT_ACTION_TO_DB += mfa.activated→mfa_activated (exhaustive satisfies holds); prisma AuditEventAction enum += mfa_activated; migration 20260614080000_add_mfa_activated (ALTER TYPE ADD VALUE IF NOT EXISTS) applied 5434 + db:generate. runtime-services AppServices += verifyMfa + MfaVerifyInput/MfaVerifyResult; LoginInput += totpCode?; createAuthServices Pick union += verifyMfa. app.ts POST /api/v1/auth/mfa/verify {code} (authed); app.test verifyMfa stub. error handler already emits {code}, so clients can branch on mfa_required vs mfa_invalid. acceptance tests/acceptance/mfa-verify-flow.test.ts (enroll→bad code 400 + stays inactive→valid code 200 activates mfaEnabledAt + /me reflects it→login no-code 401 mfa_required→login wrong-code 401 mfa_invalid→login valid-code 200). - v0.1.229 (MFA increment 3: RECOVERY CODES): single-use MFA recovery codes, hashed at rest, shown once. NEW prisma model MfaRecoveryCode (recoveryCodeId/userId FK/codeHash @unique/consumedAt?/createdAt; @@index[userId,consumedAt]) + User.mfaRecoveryCodes relation; migration 20260614090000_add_mfa_recovery_codes (CREATE TABLE + 2 ALTER TYPE ADD VALUE — combined safely since the new enum values aren't used in-migration) applied 5434 + db:generate. auth.ts: generateRecoveryCodes() → 10 codes (base32 of randomBytes(10) → 16 chars formatted XXXX-XXXX-XXXX-XXXX), store ONLY hashSecret(normalizeRecoveryCode(code)) [normalize = strip non-alnum + uppercase] — plaintext returned ONCE. verifyMfa: on FIRST activation mints + stores the 10 codes (in a $transaction with the mfaEnabledAt update) + returns recoveryCodes (re-verify returns []). LOGIN: input.recoveryCode accepted in place of totpCode when MFA active — matches an UNCONSUMED stored hash → mark consumedAt + audit mfa.recovery_used + allow; else falls through to TOTP; neither provided → 401 mfa_required; both fail → 401 mfa_invalid. NEW regenerateMfaRecoveryCodes(context,{password?,totpCode?}): RE-AUTH required (current password via argon2 OR a current TOTP) else 401 reauth_required; deletes old + issues fresh 10 (in a $transaction) + audit mfa.recovery_regenerated. POST /api/v1/auth/mfa/recovery-codes/regenerate. shared AuditEventActionSchema += mfa.recovery_used/recovery_regenerated; runtime-services AUDIT_ACTION_TO_DB + prisma AuditEventAction enum (in same migration); MfaVerifyResult += recoveryCodes; NEW RegenerateMfaRecoveryCodesInput/Result; LoginInput += recoveryCode?; AppServices += regenerateMfaRecoveryCodes + createAuthServices Pick union; app.test verifyMfa stub += recoveryCodes + regenerate stub. LoginInputSchema (app.ts) += recoveryCode optional. acceptance mfa-recovery-flow.test.ts (verify→10 codes; recovery login 200; reuse→401 mfa_invalid; totp still 200; regenerate w/ password→fresh 10; old unused code→401 after regen; new code→200; regenerate w/o re-auth→401 reauth_required). - v0.1.247 (CORE PRODUCT, increment 14 — area 1 REAL MEASURED VALIDATION, engine): NEW measured module periscan.http_redirect_enforcement (HTTP→HTTPS Redirect Enforcement Check). Survey/distinctness: http_health_check follows redirects on the HTTPS url + flags if the FINAL url is cleartext (downgrade-during-follow); it does NOT test whether the http:// entrypoint is upgraded. A site can serve HTTPS perfectly yet also serve http:// with a 200 (no redirect) → cleartext/MITM exposure. This module is genuinely distinct: it probes the CLEARTEXT http:// scheme with fetch redirect:"manual" and MEASURES whether the response is a 3xx whose Location is an https:// URL. Module: ActiveNonInvasive, single GET (NO new tool/dep — toolName "periscan-http" shared, Proprietary), executionMode ControlPlane, fixtureSupported+liveSupported, mission types ValidationSnapshot/ExposureValidation/FixVerification/ContinuousValidation, maxNetworkRequests 1. NEW probeHttpRedirect(url) (manual redirect, captures status+Location). Outcomes: 3xx→https:// → http_redirect_enforced + "Fixed" (no signal; fix-verification compatible); 2xx cleartext OR 3xx that stays http:// (no upgrade) → http_no_https_redirect + "Validated" (measured exposure) + HttpNoHttpsRedirect Exposure signal (confidence 0.9); unreachable → http_unreachable + "Inconclusive". Evidence: {httpsRedirect, location, status, url, measured:true} sensitivityLevel Low (public cleartext probe). Default fixture = 301→https (Fixed) so existing snapshot/fix-verify paths unaffected unless a test opts out. HONEST: measured from the real http:// response; absence-of-upgrade = real, re-checkable downgrade exposure. Added HttpRedirectEnforcementCheckTargetSchema (fixtureResponse{status,location?} for deterministic CI). Registered between http_cookie_security + dns_resolution_check; updated index.test EXACT moduleId array (+periscan.http_redirect_enforcement) + 4 contract tests (enforced→Fixed/no signal/httpsRedirect:true; 200 cleartext→Validated+HttpNoHttpsRedirect; redirect-staying-http→Validated+httpsRedirect:false; unreachable→Inconclusive). licenses:write (34 modules now). - v0.1.252 (CORE PRODUCT, increment 19 — area 1 REAL MEASURED VALIDATION, engine): NEW measured module periscan.well_known_security_txt (Well-Known security.txt Disclosure-Channel Check, RFC 9116). DISTINCT from the http/dns/tls posture family by PURPOSE: this is an INFORMATIONAL control-hygiene observation, NOT a security-posture/exposure check. Single passive GET https://host/.well-known/security.txt (PassiveReadOnly, toolName "periscan-http" shared, NO new tool/dep, Proprietary), executionMode ControlPlane, maxNetworkRequests 1, mission types ValidationSnapshot/ExposureValidation/ContinuousValidation. NEW probeSecurityTxt (fetch, follow redirects, body capped at 4096B). HONESTY-CRITICAL framing: present (200) → outcome security_txt_present + validationState "Detected" (control observed) + parses hasContact (RFC 9116 required) / hasExpires; absent (non-200) → security_txt_absent + validationState "Inconclusive" (NEVER "Validated" — absence is NOT an exposure); both emit a ControlObservation signal (SecurityTxtPublished / SecurityTxtMissing), NEVER an Exposure signal, so a missing disclosure channel is never over-claimed as a vulnerability. unreachable → http_unreachable + Inconclusive. Evidence: {present, hasContact, hasExpires, status, url, measured:true} sensitivity Low (public well-known file). Added WellKnownSecurityTxtCheckTargetSchema (fixtureStatus/fixtureBody/fixtureUnreachable for deterministic CI). Registered between http_redirect_enforcement + dns_resolution_check; updated index.test EXACT moduleId array (+periscan.well_known_security_txt) + 4 contract tests (present→Detected+ControlObservation+SecurityTxtPublished+hasContact/Expires + asserts NOT Exposure; absent→Inconclusive+SecurityTxtMissing + asserts NOT Validated/NOT Exposure; 200-without-Contact→Detected+hasContact:false; unreachable→Inconclusive). licenses:write (35 modules). - (v0.1.260 round — NO TAG, honest negative result) Pivoted to backend correctness per directive. Investigated a suspected serializer gap: serializeRemediationTask supports latestVerification + relatedPathEvidenceBasis, and I found buildOperatorContextForTenant's remediationTask.findMany did NOT load the attackPath relation or verification events (always-null provenance there). I implemented the fix (include attackPath + batch-load latest verification events) and it typechecked — BUT then verified consumers: (a) the USER-VISIBLE path validation-ops uses listRemediations (GET /remediations), which ALREADY correctly loads attackPath + latestVerificationEvent (services/remediation.ts L78-126); (b) NO consumer (model-gateway engine, signal-triggers) reads .latestVerification/.relatedPathEvidenceBasis/.remediations-provenance off the operator context. So the buildOperatorContextForTenant change was a LATENT consistency fix adding 2 unread DB queries per engine/signal-trigger call for data nobody consumes — a perf cost for no observable benefit. REVERTED it (git checkout) rather than ship a no-op dressed as a fix (proof-core ethos: real+tested, never over-claim). Also checked the report/closure-pack path: snapshots are point-in-time (loaded from a stored JSON artifact), so "Measured re-validations: M of N" reflects provenance AS OF snapshot generation — defensible semantics, not a bug. CONCLUSION: the paths that matter are already correct; the easy backend gaps are also thin. This + the v0.1.259 saturation note = the autonomous low-risk increment stream has reached diminishing returns. SURFACING TO USER (AskUserQuestion) for direction on the genuinely high-value remaining work, which needs a decision: (1) bless the FLAGGED heuristic-correlation validationState:"Validated" over-claim fix (real proof-core honesty fix, cross-cutting test impact); (2) a larger area-1 measured module in a new domain (e.g. TLS protocol-version weakness, multi-handshake, moderate complexity); (3) un-park billing/SSO. - v0.1.261 (CORE PRODUCT, increment 28 — area 1 REAL MEASURED VALIDATION, BIGGER module, user-blessed): NEW measured module periscan.tls_protocol_audit (TLS Protocol-Version Audit). Genuinely DISTINCT from tls_certificate_check (cert expiry/chain/hostname): this MEASURES whether the endpoint still negotiates the deprecated TLS 1.0/1.1 protocols. node:tls only (NO new tool/dep — toolName "periscan-tls" shared, Proprietary), ActiveNonInvasive, ControlPlane, maxNetworkRequests 2, mission types ValidationSnapshot/ExposureValidation/FixVerification/ContinuousValidation. NEW probeTlsProtocol(host,port,version): tls.connect pinned to minVersion=maxVersion=version; a COMPLETED handshake at that version = "supported" (unambiguous measured proof the server allows the weak protocol); everything else conservatively classified — a clear peer protocol-version alert (code ERR_SSL_TLSV1_ALERT_PROTOCOL_VERSION) = "rejected", and ANY ambiguity (client/openssl can't offer the protocol, generic conn error, timeout, sync throw) = "unmeasurable". HONESTY-CRITICAL: supported→"tls_deprecated_protocol"/"Validated"+TlsDeprecatedProtocol Exposure signal (measured:true); ALL deprecated protocols positively rejected→"tls_modern_only"/"Fixed" (measured:true); else (any unmeasurable, no support)→"tls_protocol_inconclusive"/"Inconclusive" (measured:false) — NEVER a fabricated pass/fail; Fixed only when we POSITIVELY measured rejection of both. Biased to Inconclusive on ambiguity (the only fully-reliable LIVE signal is a successful weak handshake = true positive). fixtureMode supplies per-protocol outcomes (fixtureProtocols{TLSv1,TLSv1.1}) for deterministic CI (never real network); default fixture both "rejected" (Fixed). Added TlsProtocolAuditTargetSchema. Registered after tls_certificate_check; updated index.test EXACT moduleId array (+periscan.tls_protocol_audit) + 4 contract tests (supported→Validated+signal+measured; both-rejected→Fixed+measured; unmeasurable→Inconclusive+measured:false; unreachable→Inconclusive). licenses:write (36 modules). - v0.1.262 (CORE PRODUCT, increment 29 — area 1 REAL MEASURED VALIDATION, new domain): NEW measured module periscan.http*cors_audit (HTTP CORS Policy Audit). Genuinely DISTINCT domain (cross-origin trust) — not covered by http_health (headers/transport), http_cookie, http_redirect, or security.txt. Single non-invasive GET carrying a probe Origin header (CORS_PROBE_ORIGIN), reads Access-Control-Allow-Origin/-Credentials. node:fetch only (NO new tool/dep — toolName "periscan-http" shared, Proprietary), ActiveNonInvasive, ControlPlane, maxNetworkRequests 1. HONESTY-CRITICAL exposure logic: flagged ONLY when the server REFLECTS the arbitrary probe origin (ACAO === probe origin → trusts ANY origin) OR returns "*" WITH credentials=true; a wildcard "\_" WITHOUT credentials is treated as an INTENTIONAL public-API pattern and is NOT flagged (browsers refuse credentials to "\*"). exposed → "http_cors_permissive"/"Validated"+HttpPermissiveCors Exposure signal (confidence 0.95 with creds, else 0.9); no reflection / no CORS headers → "http_cors_restricted"/"Fixed"; unreachable → "http_unreachable"/"Inconclusive". Evidence: {allowOrigin, allowCredentials, reflectsArbitraryOrigin, probeOrigin, url, measured:true} sensitivity Low. Added HttpCorsAuditTargetSchema (fixtureHeaders{accessControlAllowOrigin,accessControlAllowCredentials} for deterministic CI). NEW probeCorsHeaders(url). Registered after http_redirect_enforcement; updated index.test EXACT moduleId array (+periscan.http_cors_audit) + 5 contract tests (reflect-origin→Validated+signal+reflectsArbitraryOrigin; wildcard-no-creds→Fixed/NOT flagged; wildcard+creds→Validated; no-headers→Fixed; unreachable→Inconclusive). licenses:write (37 modules). - v0.1.299 (AUTONOMOUS, increment 66 — RUNNER STALENESS indicator, real operational gap): found a genuine gap — the control plane stamps Runner.lastSeenAt on every poll/enroll/result but NEVER derives the "Offline" status (which exists in RunnerStatusSchema) from silence, so a runner that stops checking in keeps reading "Active" with a stale timestamp; an operator can't see at a glance that an in-network agent has gone dark. Added a UI-only staleness signal (honest, no false server-status claim): pure exported helper isRunnerStale(lastSeenAt, now, thresholdMs=RUNNER_STALE_AFTER_MS=15m) (null/invalid → not stale; strictly-greater-than threshold) + a "Stale" warning badge in the runners list shown ONLY for Active/Degraded runners last seen beyond the window (Revoked/Offline/Provisioning excluded as noise). Tests: unit (isRunnerStale: long-gone=stale, just-seen=not, exactly-at-threshold=not, null/invalid=not) + component (a far-past Active runner shows "connectivity: stale", a just-polled one does not). Date.now() in component is fine; helper takes now for deterministic unit coverage. Gates GREEN: web test 132 (+2), web typecheck, web lint, web build. Web-only (no api/db) → DB suites not required. - v0.1.303 (AUTONOMOUS, increment 70 — QA: runner CREDENTIAL ROTATION coverage; clarified real semantics): covered rotateRunnerCredentials (untested sibling of revoke). VERIFIED the real behavior FIRST (avoided a false assumption): "credential rotation" rotates the CERTIFICATE + task-signing key material + recorded version — it does NOT rotate the long-lived bearer enrollment token (authTokenHash set only at enroll, never updated by rotate; issueRunnerCredentials returns cert+signing material, no new bearer token). Rotating the bearer is what REVOKE does (v0.1.302). NEW runner-credential-rotation-flow.test.ts (DB-5434): enroll (version 0.1.0, bearer token) → rotate with MISMATCHED tenantId in body → 403 runner_identity_mismatch (identity guard) → rotate with matching identity + version 0.1.1 → 200 returns fresh taskSigningKeyId/PublicKeyPem + runner.version advances to 0.1.1 → audit runner_credentials_rotated{version:0.1.1} persisted (with previousCertificateExpiresAt) → poll with the SAME bearer token still 200 (rotation refreshes cert/signing, NOT the bearer — honest assertion of the real design). Test-only (no production change). Documents the revoke-vs-rotate security distinction truthfully. Gates GREEN: -r typecheck, -r lint, test:security 22, test:acceptance 67 (+1 file, clean first run). - v0.1.328 (PROOF-CORE TRUTHFULNESS / wrong-scoped-aggregate): the executive "Fixes overdue for re-verification" metric (ValidationSnapshotMetrics.staleVerificationCount) and the snapshot summary's continuous-re-verification narrative were computed over the WRONG set. Both `apps/api/src/services/snapshots.ts` and `services/tenant.ts` (CTEM summary) called `countStaleVerifications(payload.remediations, now)`, and `buildVerificationFreshnessNote` ran over the same `payload.remediations` — but that array is built in `buildValidationSnapshotPayload` ONLY from the top-path display slice (`topAttackPaths`, capped at min(maxTopItems,10)) and only for Critical/High paths, via `ensureSnapshotRemediationForPath` which (re)creates each one HERE with a freshly-scheduled `nextVerificationAt`. So the metric almost always reported 0 overdue and the narrative claimed "All N settled fixes are within the re-verification window" — while the tenant's genuinely stale Fixed/Mitigated/PartiallyFixed remediations living outside the display slice were silently ignored. This over-claims the freshness of "Fixed" posture, the exact "is our Fixed still true?" question the metric exists to answer honestly. The Prisma `@@index([tenantId, status, nextVerificationAt])` on RemediationTask was already in place for the tenant-wide query but never used by this metric. FIX: `buildValidationSnapshotPayload` now loads the tenant-wide remediation set (`prisma.remediationTask.findMany` select status+nextVerificationAt for the tenant), computes `staleVerificationCount` from it via `countStaleVerifications`, returns it in the payload, and feeds the same tenant-wide set to `buildVerificationFreshnessNote`. Both callers now read `payload.staleVerificationCount` instead of recomputing over the embedded subset (removed the now-unused `countStaleVerifications` imports). `buildVerificationFreshnessNote` is now exported and re-typed to the minimal `{nextVerificationAt?, status}` shape and uses `SETTLED_REMEDIATION_STATUSES`. Added 4 unit tests in runtime-services.test.ts covering empty/lapsed/fresh and the tenant-wide "lapsed fix outside the slice still flagged" case. Gates GREEN: api typecheck, pnpm lint, api unit tests (231, incl new) ; shared 87 + reports 19 green. NO-DB gates only — Docker unavailable, so test:security/test:acceptance skipped (no migration/write path changed; the new query is a read). NEXT: still-open FOLLOW-UP from v0.1.326 — verify whether EDR/XDR mock-observer "Detected"/"Alerted" defaults are the same over-claim class as the SIEM "Logged" fix.

- v0.1.330 (PROOF-CORE TRUTHFULNESS / wrong-scoped-aggregate): continuing the v0.1.329 sweep across the FULL RemediationStatus enum, found the executive "Open remediations" metric still drops `Inconclusive`. `buildExecutiveTrendSummary` (apps/api/src/runtime-services.ts ~1395) computed `openRemediations` as `["Open","InProgress","PartiallyFixed","StillExposed"]` — but the 10-value enum also has `Inconclusive`, which lands in NO velocity bucket (open / readyForVerification=VerificationPending / fixed=Fixed,Mitigated / reopened=Reopened / closedWithoutEvidence=ClosedWithoutEvidence) yet still counts in `totalRemediations`. So the "Open remediations" executive metric UNDER-CLAIMED open work and the velocity breakdown failed to reconcile to the total — silently hiding inconclusive (unproven-closed) exposure. This is wrong by the codebase's own canon: an Inconclusive verification never proved the exposure closed ("the honest conclusion is Inconclusive, never Fixed", ~6069/6082/6093), `mapRemediationStatusToFindingStatus` (~4930) maps it to the active "Inconclusive" finding state, and `countOpenRemediations` (domain.ts ~1836) defines RESOLVED as Fixed/Mitigated ONLY, counting Inconclusive as open. FIX: add `"Inconclusive"` to the `openRemediations` allowlist (alongside StillExposed/PartiallyFixed, both also confirmed-not-resolved); it stays OUT of `fixedRemediations` (no over-claim of a verified fix). Added a `buildExecutiveTrendSummary` test asserting an Inconclusive remediation lands in the open_remediations metric (value+evidenceIds) and remediationVelocity.openRemediations, NOT verified_fixes, and that the breakdown reconciles (open 2 + fixed 1 + ready 0 + reopened 0 + cwe 0 == total 3); @periscan/api 232->233. Gates GREEN: api typecheck, repo lint, api unit 233. test:security/test:acceptance skipped — Docker unavailable, pure aggregate helper, no DB paths touched. NEXT: the enum sweep is now exhausted for this metric (all 10 statuses bucketed); VARY to another subsystem's aggregate or a set-but-not-enforced field.

- v0.1.331 (PROOF-CORE TRUTHFULNESS / over-claimed-proof — closes the v0.1.326+v0.1.328 EDR/XDR follow-up for the XDR case): the Microsoft Defender XDR mock control observer defaulted an absent/unrecognized `config.fixtureOutcome` to the POSITIVE detection verdict `"Alerted"` — over-claiming a successful detection with no supporting evidence — even though it shares the SIEM `Alerted`/`Logged`/`NoEvidence` vocabulary and every SIEM sibling (splunk/elastic-security/datadog-siem/google-chronicle/sumo-logic/rapid7-insightidr/ibm-qradar) was already fixed in v0.1.220/v0.1.326 to default to the neutral `"Logged"`. `normalizeMicrosoftDefenderXdrOutcome` (packages/connectors/src/index.ts ~415) was the lone holdout still returning `"Alerted"` in its default branch. The LIVE observer (`observeMicrosoftDefenderXdrControl` ~14747) correctly sets `outcome: hasEvidence ? "Alerted" : "NoEvidence"` directly and never touches the normalize default, so live detection truth is unchanged; only the mock default over-claimed. FIX: change the default branch from `"Alerted"` to `"Logged"` (telemetry recorded, no alert claimed), matching the shared SIEM convention this XDR's vocabulary belongs to. Extended packages/connectors/src/siem-mock-observer-default.test.ts with a `microsoft-defender-xdr` describe block (missing fixtureOutcome -> "Logged" not "Alerted"; unrecognized -> "Logged"; explicit "Alerted" still honored). Gates GREEN: @periscan/connectors test 221 (was 218; +3 new), connectors typecheck, api typecheck, repo lint. NO-DB gates only — Docker unavailable, pure connector outcome-normalizer, no DB/migration/write paths touched, so test:security/test:acceptance skipped. PARTIALLY-REMAINING FOLLOW-UP: the pure-EDR/WAF normalizers (crowdstrike/sentinelone/vmware-carbon-black/sophos/trend-micro/palo-cortex-xdr + fastly/akamai/imperva WAF + panorama/fortigate/zscaler-zia) still default to `"Detected"`; their vocab has NO "Logged", so the correct neutral default is "NoEvidence" vs "Missed" — still needs a contract decision before a sweep (deliberately NOT blind-fixed here, same honest-scope reasoning as v0.1.326). NEXT: define the EDR/WAF neutral-mock contract, then sweep those 12 defaults; OR rotate to a fresh aggregate/set-but-not-enforced area.

- v0.1.332 (PROOF-CORE TRUTHFULNESS / set-but-not-enforced — runner-task lifecycle state machine): rotated off the connector/executive-metric vein to the internal-runner result-ingestion path. `submitRunnerTaskResult` (apps/api/src/services/runner.ts) gated only on runner identity and TIME-based expiry before overwriting the validation run's `outcome`, `validationState`, and `evidenceIds` and attaching the runner-uploaded evidence — but it never checked the task's CURRENT lifecycle state, even though its siblings `acceptRunnerTask` (acceptableStates `Queued`/`Leased`) and `rejectRunnerTask` (terminalStates guard) both do. So a task that had already reached a TERMINAL state could still post a result and mutate the run: (a) double-submit/replay of an already-`Completed` task re-overwrote the settled run; (b) more seriously, a task the runner had `DeniedByLocalPolicy`/`DeniedByServerPolicy`'d or that was `Cancelled`/`Failed`/`Expired` could RESURRECT itself with a fresh `Completed` outcome + evidence + validationState — a denied/cancelled task producing proof, directly against "denied tasks must never execute" and "a risk cannot be marked fixed without a real verification event". FIX: extracted a pure module-level predicate `isTerminalRunnerTaskStatus(status)` over the closed RunnerTaskStatus states (Cancelled/Completed/DeniedByLocalPolicy/DeniedByServerPolicy/Expired/Failed/Rejected) and added a guard in `submitRunnerTaskResult` AFTER the identity check and BEFORE the time-expiry branch: a result from a terminal-state task now writes a `runner.task.rejected` audit event (reason `result_after_terminal_state`, with state) and throws `AppServiceError(409, "runner_task_invalid_state")` — the same error code/shape accept/reject use. In-flight states (Queued/Leased/Running/Accepted) are unaffected, so the live single-submit happy path is unchanged; the existing app.test.ts resubmit case hits the identity-mismatch 403 (wrong runnerId) before this guard, so it is unaffected. Added apps/api/src/runner-task-result-state.test.ts (3 unit tests: all terminal states true, in-flight states + unknown false). Gates GREEN: @periscan/api typecheck (after prisma generate), repo lint, api unit tests 236 (was 233; +3 new file). NO-DB gates only — Docker unavailable, so test:security/test:acceptance skipped; this DOES touch a DB-write path, but the change is a pre-write state guard reusing the existing error contract and audit event, and the lone DB-gated submit-twice test in app.test.ts is shielded by the earlier identity check. NEXT: with DB available, run test:security + test:acceptance to confirm the runner result-ingestion acceptance flow; OR continue the set-but-not-enforced sweep on other lifecycle transitions (e.g. uploadRunnerTaskArtifact / poll lease state guards).

- v0.1.333 (PROOF-CORE TRUTHFULNESS / over-claimed-proof — executes the EDR/WAF mock-observer-default sweep explicitly flagged as the PARTIALLY-REMAINING FOLLOW-UP in the v0.1.331 entry): the 12 pure EDR/WAF/network-control mock outcome normalizers in packages/connectors/src/index.ts (`normalizeCrowdStrikeOutcome`, `normalizeSentinelOneOutcome`, `normalizeCarbonBlackOutcome`, `normalizeSophosOutcome`, `normalizeTrendMicroOutcome`, `normalizePaloAltoCortexXdrOutcome`, `normalizeFastlyWafOutcome`, `normalizeAkamaiWafOutcome`, `normalizeImpervaWafOutcome`, `normalizePanoramaOutcome`, `normalizeFortiGateOutcome`, `normalizeZscalerZiaOutcome`) all defaulted an absent/unrecognized `config.fixtureOutcome` to the POSITIVE detection verdict `"Detected"` — over-claiming a successful EDR/WAF detection with no supporting evidence (same family of bug as the SIEM/XDR fixes in v0.1.220/v0.1.326/v0.1.331), and the shared normalizer also drives the fixture LOADERS, so an unconfigured mock additionally FABRICATED a sample detection signal in collectSignals. CONTRACT DECISION (the open question from v0.1.331): the correct neutral default is the verdict each connector's LIVE observer returns when a query succeeds but finds no matching evidence — verified per-observer: all 11 of SentinelOne/CarbonBlack/Sophos/TrendMicro/Cortex-XDR/Fastly/Akamai/Imperva/Panorama/FortiGate/Zscaler return `"NoEvidence"`, while CrowdStrike Falcon (controlObservationCapabilities `["Detected","Blocked","Missed"]`, NO `"NoEvidence"` verdict) returns `"Missed"`. FIX: changed each default branch `"Detected"` -> the matching neutral (`"NoEvidence"` for the 11, `"Missed"` for crowdstrike). This is consistent in BOTH paths: (a) `observeControl` mock verdict no longer over-claims a detection; (b) the fixture loaders already treat `"Missed"`/`"NoEvidence"` as no-detection (return `[]` / `num_found:0`), so an unconfigured mock now emits an honest no-evidence telemetry state instead of a fabricated detection — matching the Real-First "honest empty/not-configured state" rule. An explicitly-configured `fixtureOutcome` (Detected/Blocked/etc.) still passes through unchanged. Added packages/connectors/src/edr-waf-mock-observer-default.test.ts (36 tests: each of the 11 defaults missing+unrecognized fixtureOutcome -> "NoEvidence" not "Detected", and still honors explicit "Detected"; crowdstrike -> "Missed" not "Detected", honors explicit "Detected"). Updated the 3 collectSignals contract tests that relied on the old over-claiming default to fabricate a detection (crowdstrike.contract.test.ts mock-ingest case + connector-correlation-hints.test.ts CarbonBlack/Cortex-XDR) to explicitly set `fixtureOutcome: "Detected"` — same signal-shape assertions, now explicit about the configured outcome. Gates GREEN: @periscan/connectors test 257 (incl. 36 new), connectors typecheck, api typecheck (after prisma generate), repo lint, api unit tests 236. NO-DB gates only — Docker unavailable, so test:security/test:acceptance skipped; pure connector outcome-normalizer change, no DB/migration/write paths touched, and the api suite mocks connectors with its own stub so it is unaffected. This CLOSES the EDR/WAF case of the SIEM/XDR mock-observer over-claim family. NEXT: rotate to a fresh aggregate/set-but-not-enforced area, or with DB available run test:security + test:acceptance for connector-backed control-validation flows.
